Confirmed — `main------` is just shorthand for `ozvrpkrx` (6 generations back from `main`). Both queries point at the same commit; jj revset syntax is dense like that.

And the answer matches the prediction exactly:

| Commit | Conflict surface |
|---|---|
| `ozvrpkrx` (0.42.0-0, oldest) | 3 files: `Cargo.toml`, `Cargo.lock`, `notes/todo.md` |
| ... `ttzwvpoq`, `mvusyowm` ... | adds `src/scope.rs`, `src/init.rs` along the way |
| `uxymmmyn` (main, tip) | 6 files cumulative |

Resolving from the bottom up means starting with the smallest surface (3 files in `ozvrpkrx`).

**These are real merge decisions, not mechanical:**
- **`Cargo.toml`** — `ozvrpkrx` bumps `0.41.0 → 0.42.0-0`. New base is `0.41.1`. Probably want `0.41.1 → 0.42.0-0` (since 0.41.1 already shipped between the original divergence and now).
- **`Cargo.lock`** — follows `Cargo.toml` version.
- **`notes/todo.md`** — `ozvrpkrx` moved 0.41.0-era Done entries to `done.md` (the "retire Done" half of its title). icr's todo.md has the new 0.41.1 close-out structure. We'll want to apply the same pattern: retire 0.41.1 Done entries to `done.md` as part of opening 0.42.0.

**Two ways to resolve in jj:**

**A) Direct edit** — `jj edit -r ozvrpkrx` to move `@` into the conflicted commit, fix files in working copy, then advance. Shortest path.

**B) New + squash** (jj's official hint) — `jj new ozvrpkrx`, fix files in a fresh empty child commit, then `jj squash` to fold the resolution back into `ozvrpkrx`. Slightly more conservative — easier to abandon if the resolution turns out wrong.

Either works. Mild lean toward (A) for less ceremony; (B) is the safer-feeling default if you'd rather have an extra abandon-able layer.

Which?

> DEVELOPER

I like B feels consistent with our conservative approach. Obviously, we were overly conservative especially since we want ochid to be valid and main-2 wouldn't have helped with that, but it still felt good. I assume even if we do (A) there will be "auto-rebasing" or "manual-rebasing"?

> AGENT

Yes — and it's automatic, not manual, in jj.

**With either (A) or (B), descendants auto-rebase as soon as the parent is modified.** This is one of jj's defining design points: any time you mutate a commit, jj automatically re-applies descendants onto the modified version. No manual `git rebase --continue` ceremony.

What that **doesn't** guarantee is that descendants become conflict-free. Auto-rebase just means "try the patch on the new parent." If the descendant's diff was conflicting because of *its own changes* (not because of the parent's broken state), the conflict persists.

**Concretely for our chain:**

After we resolve `ozvrpkrx` (Cargo.toml, Cargo.lock, todo.md):
- jj auto-rebases `ttzwvpoq` (0.42.0-1, Scope enum) onto the new ozvrpkrx.
- ttzwvpoq's own diff bumps `Cargo.toml` `0.42.0-0 → 0.42.0-1`. Applies cleanly to our resolved 0.42.0-0. **No conflict on Cargo.toml at ttzwvpoq.**
- ttzwvpoq introduces `src/scope.rs` changes. These conflict with icr's `src/scope.rs` removals (ttzwvpoq added 236 lines, icr removed 29). **Likely still conflicted on src/scope.rs.**
- And so on up the chain — Cargo.toml conflicts likely clear (just version-step diffs from the resolved base), but src/init.rs and src/scope.rs and CLAUDE.md will remain conflicted because those are real overlapping authored conflicts.

So the workflow is: resolve `ozvrpkrx`, check `jj resolve --list -r main` again, see what cleared and what remains, walk up to the next still-conflicted commit, repeat.

Going with (B). Proposed command:

```
jj new ozvrpkrx -R .
```

This creates a new empty commit on top of `ozvrpkrx`. `@` moves there; the working copy fills with `ozvrpkrx`'s tree (including the 3 conflicted files with jj conflict markers). We edit, then `jj squash` folds the resolution back into `ozvrpkrx`.

A few candidate names:

- **`conflict-base`** — describes the role (bottom of the conflict cascade we're working through). Self-documenting.
- **`420-0-rebased`** — version-anchored. Says exactly what's at this commit.
- **`bottom`** — shortest; a bit too generic (`jj log` reader wouldn't know what it means).

Mild lean toward `conflict-base` for self-documentation. Will be deleted at the end alongside `gca-icr-main`, `main-2`, etc.

Bookmark behavior here: it tracks chid `ozvrpkrx`. When we squash the resolution into the commit (modifying it), the bookmark stays at `ozvrpkrx` (now with new cid). When we eventually advance up the chain or finalize, we delete the bookmark.

Proposed command:

```
jj bookmark create conflict-base -r ozvrpkrx -R .
```

Approve, or different name?

> DEVELOPER

How about rslv-commit or rslv-cmt

> AGENT

Both work. Brief comparison:

- **`rslv-commit`** — clearer (`commit` is unambiguous). 11 chars.
- **`rslv-cmt`** — shorter (8 chars). `cmt` could read as "commitment" / "commute" / "comment" out of context.

Since the bookmark name appears in `jj log` output (not just in command typing), legibility there nudges me toward `rslv-commit`. Three extra characters cost little when typing.

Also worth thinking through usage pattern — this bookmark is essentially a **cursor** for "commit currently being resolved." As we advance up the chain (after squashing the resolution into `ozvrpkrx`, we move to `ttzwvpoq`, etc.), we can either:
- **Keep the same bookmark and move it**: `jj bookmark set rslv-commit -r ttzwvpoq` — one stable name, moves with us.
- **Delete and recreate per commit**: more ceremony.

Lean toward keeping one bookmark and advancing it.

Mild lean on name: **`rslv-commit`**. Your call though — `rslv-cmt` works too if brevity wins.

Proposed command (with `rslv-commit`):

```
jj bookmark create rslv-commit -r ozvrpkrx -R .
```

Approve, or switch to `rslv-cmt`?

> AGENT

Sure. jj's conflict format is asymmetric on purpose — one side shown as a diff, the other as a snapshot — to keep complex conflicts readable. Walking through the markers:

```
<<<<<<< conflict 1 of 1                                            ← start marker
%%%%%%% diff from: ykxrlkkw 6747a27b "...0.41.0..." (parents of rebased revision)   ← BASE
\\\\\\\        to: slvprlpw 6c1302fb "...0.41.1..." (rebase destination)             ← SIDE A header
-version = "0.41.0"                                                ← BASE had this
+version = "0.41.1"                                                ← SIDE A changed it to this
+++++++ ozvrpkrx 1bb61bb7 "...0.42.0-0..." (rebased revision)      ← SIDE B header
version = "0.42.0-0"                                               ← SIDE B's literal snapshot
>>>>>>> conflict 1 of 1 ends                                       ← end marker
```

**Three actors, three values for this line:**

| Side | Role | Value |
|---|---|---|
| BASE (`ykxrlkkw`) | The parent the rebased commit *was* based on (0.41.0 close-out) | `version = "0.41.0"` |
| **SIDE A** (`slvprlpw`) | The rebase destination (icr's tip — what we're rebasing onto) | `version = "0.41.1"` (it bumped from BASE) |
| **SIDE B** (`ozvrpkrx`) | The commit being rebased | `version = "0.42.0-0"` |

**Marker conventions:**
- `%%%%%%%` opens a **diff section** — the line below is a unified diff (with `-` and `+` lines) showing how that side differs from BASE. Compact for small changes.
- `+++++++` opens a **snapshot section** — the line below is the literal content of that side. Used when the side's content is easier read whole than as a diff.
- `\\\\\\\` is just the second line of the diff section header (the "to: ..." continuation).

**Why the asymmetry?** jj could have shown both sides as diffs:
```
... slvprlpw diff:
-version = "0.41.0"
+version = "0.41.1"
... ozvrpkrx diff:
-version = "0.41.0"
+version = "0.42.0-0"
```

Or both as snapshots:
```
slvprlpw: version = "0.41.1"
ozvrpkrx: version = "0.42.0-0"
```

But jj picks per-side: one diff + one snapshot is often most readable when one side is a large substantive change (best as snapshot) and the other is a small tweak (best as diff). For this trivial single-line conflict it's overkill, but the format is the same regardless of conflict size.

**Conflict semantics:** BASE had a value, SIDE A changed it one way, SIDE B changed it differently. Auto-merge can't pick — both modified the same line in different ways. We pick which value we want and replace the whole `<<<<<<< ... >>>>>>>` block (lines 3 through 10 in your file) with that single line.

For Cargo.toml, the right answer is `version = "0.42.0-0"` (we want ozvrpkrx's cycle-opening intent on the new base).

Make sense? Ready to apply, or more decoding first?

- `../vc-x1-main/` at `rslv-commit` (ttzwvpoq, cid `a018e175`) → **main side** of the merge: what ttzwvpoq originally produced before the rebase. Untouched original tree.
- `../vc-x1-icr/` at `icr` (slvprlpw, init-clone-refactor tip) → **new-base side**: what icr's tip leaves at each path.
- Our vc-x1 working copy → **3-way merge in progress**: jj has tried to replay ttzwvpoq's diff onto icr and surfaced conflicts.

So for any file with a conflict here, I can read all three:
1. `vc-x1/<file>` — conflict markers
2. `../vc-x1-main/<file>` — clean main-side content
3. `../vc-x1-icr/<file>` — clean icr-side content

That's a strict superset of staring at conflict markers. The two snapshot blocks (`+++++++ Contents of side #1` / `+++++++ Contents of side #2`) in jj's format should each match one of the reference trees exactly. If they don't, we know jj has compressed one side into a diff form and we read the reference tree to get the literal.

One operational note: the `jj new rslv-commit` empty WCs in the reference clones are harmless and don't affect what files-on-disk show — those reflect the parent commit's tree. So you don't need to manage those WCs as we go.

Cadence going forward: as we squash + advance `rslv-commit` here (ttzwvpoq → mvusyowm → …), you advance `rslv-commit` in `../vc-x1-main/` to the same chid. `icr` in `../vc-x1-icr/` stays at `slvprlpw` for the whole rebase — icr is the new base, doesn't move. Sound right?

Read complete. One conflict region in `init.rs` at lines 560-622. Decoding what I see:

**Format key:**
- 561-593: snapshot of "rebase destination" (= the new parent: resolved ozvrpkrx, which is icr's reshape + version bump). This is **icr-side content** at this region.
- 594-621: 3-way diff showing how ttzwvpoq's original parent (pre-rebase 1bb61bb7 ozvrpkrx) → ttzwvpoq itself differed. This is **ttzwvpoq's diff** in its original setting.

**The two sides:**

icr-side (`plan_init`) uses 2-arg signature with `cfg: &UserConfig` and matches on `args.scope.scope` (a `ScopeKind` enum) with branches:
- `ScopeKind::CodeBot => Scope(vec![Side::Code, Side::Bot])`
- `ScopeKind::Por => Scope(vec![Side::Code])`

ttzwvpoq's diff (in pre-rebase setting) renames `Scope(vec![...])` → `Scope::Roles(vec![...])` — the "Scope enum refactor" introducing variants. It was matching against `&args.scope` (None/Some) in the old code shape.

**The conflict:** ttzwvpoq's intent (rename Scope tuple-struct calls to `Scope::Roles` enum-variant calls) needs to apply to icr's new plan_init shape. The icr shape *already* has `Scope(...)` call sites — just inside different match arms. So the resolution is mechanical at the call sites.

**Hesitation:** ttzwvpoq's commit title is "Scope enum (Roles + Single)". For the `ScopeKind::Por` arm (single side), do we want `Scope::Roles(vec![Side::Code])` or `Scope::Single(Side::Code)`? The literal-rename answer (Roles for both) is safe; the conceptually-cleaner answer (Single for Por) presumes a redesign decision ttzwvpoq's diff doesn't show.

I'd like to read the Scope enum definition (in `src/scope.rs`) before committing to this — it'll tell us exactly what variants exist and which is right for the Por case. The Scope enum content lives at the *next* conflicted file we were going to look at anyway.

**Proposed reorder:** read `src/scope.rs`'s conflict next instead of resolving `src/init.rs` first. The Scope enum design there determines the right `init.rs` resolution.

1	//! Which side(s) of a workspace a command should operate on.
2	//!
3	//! - `Side` is the clap `ValueEnum` — one of `code`, `bot`.
4	//! - `Scope` is an enum:
5	//!   - `Roles(Vec<Side>)` — dual-repo workspace; the parsed
6	//!     keyword form (`code`, `bot`, `code,bot`, `bot,code`).
7	//!   - `Single(PathBuf)` — explicit single-repo mode; ignores
8	//!     `.vc-config.toml` and operates on the one repo at the
9	//!     given path. Wired up incrementally (parser lands in
10	//!     0.42.0-2, consumers in later steps).
11	//! - Helpers (`has_code`, `is_both`, etc.) reflect the `Roles`
12	//!   arm only; on `Single(_)` they all return `false` /
13	//!   `is_empty()` returns `false`. Callers that must
14	//!   distinguish modes match on the enum directly.
15	//! - See `notes/chores-07.md > --scope enum refactor (0.42.0)`
16	//!   for the migration plan, and `notes/chores-06.md > 0.41.0-4`
17	//!   for the vocabulary capture.

19	use clap::ValueEnum;
20	use std::path::PathBuf;
21	
22	/// One side of a dual-repo workspace.
23	///
24	/// - `Code` — the primary (app) repo.
25	/// - `Bot` — the Claude Code session repo (typically at `.claude/`).
26	#[derive(Copy, Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, ValueEnum)]
27	pub enum Side {
28	    Code,
29	    Bot,
30	}
31	
32	/// Parsed `--scope` value.
33	///
34	/// - `Roles(_)` — dual-repo workspace; the requested side set.
35	/// - `Single(_)` — explicit single-repo mode at the given path.
36	#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
37	pub enum Scope {
38	    Roles(Vec<Side>),
39	    // Staged: production code starts constructing this in 0.42.0-2
40	    // (parser) / -3 (sync resolver); only tests reach it in -1.
41	    #[allow(dead_code)]
42	    Single(PathBuf),
43	}

1	//! Which side(s) of a workspace a command should operate on.
2	//!
3	//! - `Side` is the clap `ValueEnum` — one of `code`, `bot`.
4	//! - `Scope` is an enum:
5	//!   - `Roles(Vec<Side>)` — dual-repo workspace; the parsed
6	//!     keyword form (`code`, `bot`, `code,bot`, `bot,code`).
7	//!   - `Single(PathBuf)` — explicit single-repo mode; ignores
8	//!     `.vc-config.toml` and operates on the one repo at the
9	//!     given path. Wired up incrementally (parser lands in
10	//!     0.42.0-2, consumers in later steps).
11	//! - Helpers (`has_code`, `is_both`, etc.) reflect the `Roles`
12	//!   arm only; on `Single(_)` they all return `false` /
13	//!   `is_empty()` returns `false`. Callers that must
14	//!   distinguish modes match on the enum directly.
15	//! - See `notes/chores-07.md > --scope enum refactor (0.42.0)`
16	//!   for the migration plan, and `notes/chores-06.md > 0.41.0-4`
17	//!   for the vocabulary capture.
18	
19	use clap::ValueEnum;
20	use std::path::PathBuf;
21	
22	/// One side of a dual-repo workspace.
23	///
24	/// - `Code` — the primary (app) repo.
25	/// - `Bot` — the Claude Code session repo (typically at `.claude/`).
26	#[derive(Copy, Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, ValueEnum)]
27	pub enum Side {
28	    Code,
29	    Bot,
30	}
31	
32	/// Parsed `--scope` value.
33	///
34	/// - `Roles(_)` — dual-repo workspace; the requested side set.
35	/// - `Single(_)` — explicit single-repo mode at the given path.
36	#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
37	pub enum Scope {
38	    Roles(Vec<Side>),
39	    // Staged: production code starts constructing this in 0.42.0-2
40	    // (parser) / -3 (sync resolver); only tests reach it in -1.
41	    #[allow(dead_code)]
42	    Single(PathBuf),
43	}
44	
45	impl Scope {
46	    /// True when the `Roles` arm includes the code side.
47	    pub fn has_code(&self) -> bool {
48	        matches!(self, Scope::Roles(v) if v.contains(&Side::Code))
49	    }
50	
51	    /// True when the `Roles` arm includes the bot side.
52	    pub fn has_bot(&self) -> bool {
53	        matches!(self, Scope::Roles(v) if v.contains(&Side::Bot))
54	    }
55	
56	    /// Roles-only with exactly the code side — single-side dual-repo op.
57	    pub fn is_code_only(&self) -> bool {
58	        self.has_code() && !self.has_bot()
59	    }
60	
61	    /// Roles-only with exactly the bot side — single-side dual-repo op.
62	    pub fn is_bot_only(&self) -> bool {
63	        !self.has_code() && self.has_bot()
64	    }
65	
66	    /// Roles arm including both sides — full dual-repo op.
67	    pub fn is_both(&self) -> bool {
68	        self.has_code() && self.has_bot()
69	    }
70	
71	    /// Roles arm with an empty side list — always invalid input.
72	    /// `Single(_)` returns `false` (it carries a path, not a side list).
73	    pub fn is_empty(&self) -> bool {
74	        matches!(self, Scope::Roles(v) if v.is_empty())
75	    }
76	}
77	
78	#[cfg(test)]
79	mod tests {
80	    use super::*;
81	
82	    #[test]
83	    fn code_only() {
84	        let s = Scope::Roles(vec![Side::Code]);
85	        assert!(s.is_code_only());
86	        assert!(!s.is_bot_only());
87	        assert!(!s.is_both());
88	    }
89	
90	    #[test]

92	        let s = Scope::Roles(vec![Side::Bot]);
93	        assert!(!s.is_code_only());
94	        assert!(s.is_bot_only());
95	        assert!(!s.is_both());
96	    }
97	
98	    #[test]
99	    fn both_code_then_bot() {
100	        let s = Scope::Roles(vec![Side::Code, Side::Bot]);
101	        assert!(!s.is_code_only());
102	        assert!(!s.is_bot_only());
103	        assert!(s.is_both());
104	    }
105	
106	    #[test]
107	    fn both_bot_then_code() {
108	        // Order doesn't matter — contains-based checks.

110	        assert!(s.is_both());
111	    }
112

1	//! Which side(s) of a workspace a command should operate on.
2	//!
3	//! - `Side` is the clap `ValueEnum` — one of `code`, `bot`.
4	//! - `Scope` wraps the parsed list; a missing flag (None at the arg
5	//!   layer) means "caller decides", typically via workspace-state
6	//!   default resolution.
7	//! - See `notes/chores-06.md > Generalize --scope across commands
8	//!   (0.40.0)` for the full rationale and the vocabulary history.

10	use clap::ValueEnum;

12	/// One side of the workspace.

14	/// - `Code` — the primary (app) repo.
15	/// - `Bot` — the Claude Code session repo (typically at `.claude/`).
16	#[derive(Copy, Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, ValueEnum)]
17	pub enum Side {
18	    Code,
19	    Bot,
20	}

> AGENT

Read complete. Both reference clones are illuminating.

**Scope enum design (from the working copy, auto-merged unconflicted parts):**

```rust
pub enum Scope {
    Roles(Vec<Side>),
    #[allow(dead_code)]
    Single(PathBuf),
}
```

Module doc says: `Single(PathBuf)` is **explicit single-repo at a path**; "Wired up incrementally (parser lands in 0.42.0-2, consumers in later steps)." So `Single` is *only constructed* starting at the next commit (mvusyowm); ttzwvpoq itself only uses `Roles`.

**Why scope.rs has 4 conflict regions:**

- `../vc-x1-icr/src/scope.rs` (72 lines) — simpler tuple struct `Scope(Vec<Side>)`. Only `is_code_only` + `is_both` helpers. Only 3 tests. icr **removed** `is_bot_only`, `is_empty`, the `bot_only` test, and the `empty` test.
- `../vc-x1-main/src/scope.rs` (135 lines) — full enum with `Roles + Single`. All 5 helpers (`is_bot_only` and `is_empty` redefined enum-aware). All 6 tests including new `single_helpers_all_false`.

The 4 conflicts are exactly the regions where icr removed lines (is_bot_only / is_empty / the bot_only and empty tests) and ttzwvpoq's diff modified those same regions for the enum redefinition.

**Resolution decision:** ttzwvpoq's *purpose* is "Scope enum (Roles + Single)" — introduce the enum and redefine the helpers. icr's removals were a separate cleanup not relevant to ttzwvpoq's work. The post-rebase intent is clearly ttzwvpoq's full design. So **take ttzwvpoq's content for all 4 conflicts**.

Net effect: the resolved `vc-x1/src/scope.rs` should be **byte-identical to `../vc-x1-main/src/scope.rs`** (135 lines). The unconflicted parts already match.

**Proposed action — replace `vc-x1/src/scope.rs` with the content of `../vc-x1-main/src/scope.rs`.** The simplest reliable way:

```
cp ../vc-x1-main/src/scope.rs src/scope.rs
```

This preserves ttzwvpoq's intended file exactly. After the cp, `jj resolve --list -r rslv-commit -R .` will drop scope.rs from the conflicted list (only via squash, which we do later).

Open you to alternatives if you'd rather edit the conflict markers in place — but `cp` is mechanical, transparent, and I can verify by re-reading the result.

Approve `cp ../vc-x1-main/src/scope.rs src/scope.rs`?

> DEVELOPER

I agree 100%, but obviously we'll going to be running our pre-flight procedures (clippy, fmt, test ...) and installing the latest version after resolving the conflict, any push back?

> AGENT

Two pushbacks, both small:

**1. Order within ttzwvpoq.** We can't run the cycle right after just scope.rs — `init.rs` and `todo.md` still have conflict markers, so `cargo build` will fail on conflict syntax (the `<<<<<<<` and `>>>>>>>` lines aren't valid Rust). The cycle has to run *after all 3 files at ttzwvpoq are resolved*, not after each one. So the per-commit rhythm is:

   1. `jj new rslv-commit -R .` (fresh WC, fills with markers)
   2. Resolve all conflicts in WC (scope.rs → init.rs → todo.md)
   3. `jj resolve --list -R .` — verify zero markers
   4. **Cargo cycle on WC** — verifies this commit's resolved state
   5. `jj squash -R .` — fold WC into rslv-commit
   6. Advance `rslv-commit` to next still-conflicted commit
   7. Repeat

**2. `cargo install --locked` per intermediate commit is overkill.** It's heavy (full release build, minutes) and doesn't add validation beyond what `test` already gives at intermediate commits — we're not using ttzwvpoq's binary; it's a transient mid-rebase state. Suggest:

   - **Per commit (1–6):** `cargo fmt` + `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings` + `cargo test`
   - **At final tip only (after all 6 squashed):** add `cargo install --path . --locked` + retest, then proceed to force-push.

That keeps every intermediate commit buildable and tested (so bisection works) without burning install-time 6×.

**Side effect to watch:** after each `jj squash`, descendants auto-rebase. Some downstream conflicts may *clear* (the rebase-status.md notes this is expected). So after each squash, re-run `jj log -r 'gca-icr-main::' ... CONFLICT ...` to see how many conflicted commits actually remain — could be fewer than 5.

If both points land right, current state of play:

- Next: read **init.rs's conflict in detail** to plan its resolution (we have a tentative plan but I want to verify it against the icr/main references at this region before editing).
- Then: read **todo.md's conflict** (rescope the 0.42.0 ladder + apply [72]→[76] renumbering).
- Then: do all 3 resolutions, cargo cycle (fmt/clippy/test), squash. One commit done.

Shall I propose the init.rs read window next?

Got it — misread you. You meant "all conflicts at this commit" (the 3-file set), not "this single file." That's exactly the rhythm I sketched in the numbered list. We're aligned: edit all 3 files → cargo cycle → squash → advance.

And on `cargo install --locked` per commit: your call. If you want install at each, I'll run it — it costs minutes per cycle but does verify the locked-deps install path against each intermediate. No further pushback.

- Take icr-side `plan_init` shape: 2-arg signature with `cfg: &UserConfig`, doc comment listing target dispatch, `match args.scope.scope` on `ScopeKind::CodeBot` / `ScopeKind::Por`.

> AGENT

Sound plan. The "don't save on exit" rule covers the main risk — meld is read-mostly and won't take exclusive locks, so it won't block our edits or jj operations.

One thing to be aware of: when we modify files (`cp`, `Edit` tool, etc.), meld's open buffers become stale. Meld usually detects external file changes and prompts you to reload — **say yes**. Reject the prompt or use "Discard your changes" rather than "Keep mine" if it asks; "Keep mine" would push meld's stale buffer back to disk and undo our edit.

Other than that, meld is just another lens — fine to leave running. After the upcoming `cp ../vc-x1-main/src/scope.rs src/scope.rs`, meld will likely show the file changed; that's our resolution landing.

2. **icr's only `Scope(...)` calls are exactly the two we already see** at icr lines 590-591 (the `ScopeKind::CodeBot` / `ScopeKind::Por` match arms). Nothing else needs renaming.

3. **icr's plan_init signature spans 4 lines** (575: `pub(crate) fn plan_init(`, 576: `    args: &InitArgs,`, 577: `    cfg: &UserConfig,`, 578: `) -> Result<...>`). Matches the snapshot in our WC's conflict (lines 577-580). 

4. **Tests:** icr's tests use 2-arg form `plan_init(&args, &cfg_...)` (~30 call sites). main's tests use 1-arg form `plan_init(&args)` (~22 call sites). Our WC's tests auto-merged to icr's 2-arg form (the auto-merge was clean because main's tests don't construct `Scope(...)` directly — they set up `args.scope` as the old `Option<Vec<Side>>`, so ttzwvpoq's `Scope(...)` → `Scope::Roles(...)` rename had no test-region overlap with icr's complete test rewrite).

**init.rs resolution plan confirmed:** replace conflict block lines 560-622 with icr's snapshot content (lines 561-593 of the conflict block), with `Scope(vec![...])` → `Scope::Roles(vec![...])` at the two arm sites:

```rust
/// Build an `InitPlan` from CLI args + user config.
///
/// Dispatches on the parsed `<TARGET>` form:
///
/// - `Url(u)` / `OwnerName(o, n)` → URL is explicit; config not
///   consulted; `--account` and `--repo` are rejected as
///   meaningless.
/// - `Path(p)` → `p` is the destination; basename names the repo;
///   remote URL resolved from config via the `--repo` chain.
/// - `BareName(n)` → destination at `cwd/<n>`; remote resolved
///   from config.
///
/// The `cfg` parameter is the loaded user config; `init` loads it
/// once at entry and passes it through so tests can supply a
/// synthetic config without touching disk.
pub(crate) fn plan_init(
    args: &InitArgs,
    cfg: &UserConfig,
) -> Result<InitPlan, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
    debug!(
        "init args: target={:?}, name={:?}, account={:?}, repo={:?}, scope={:?}, private={}",
        args.target,
        args.name,
        args.account.account,
        args.repo.repo,
        args.scope.scope,
        args.provision.private.private
    );

    let scope = match args.scope.scope {
        ScopeKind::CodeBot => Scope::Roles(vec![Side::Code, Side::Bot]),
        ScopeKind::Por => Scope::Roles(vec![Side::Code]),
```

Line 623 `    };` (auto-merged) flows right after; rest of plan_init body is icr's content unchanged.

Neither pure side — it's a hybrid. To be precise:

**Structure (signature, doc comment, match dispatch): icr's side wins.**
- icr's 2-arg `plan_init(args, cfg: &UserConfig)`
- icr's doc comment about target dispatch
- icr's `match args.scope.scope` on the `ScopeKind` enum (`CodeBot` / `Por` arms)

**Scope construction calls: main/ttzwvpoq's rename intent wins.**
- icr writes `Scope(vec![Side::Code, Side::Bot])` (tuple-struct form)
- ttzwvpoq's whole purpose is "Scope enum (Roles + Single)" — the rename `Scope(...)` → `Scope::Roles(...)`
- We apply that rename to icr's two construction sites: `Scope::Roles(vec![Side::Code, Side::Bot])` and `Scope::Roles(vec![Side::Code])`

**Dropped from main/ttzwvpoq's side:**
- The old `Some(sides)` arm with `is_empty()` / `is_bot_only()` validation. Those checks were guards against the old `Option<Vec<Side>>` arg shape where `--scope` was a free-form list. In icr's `ScopeKind` enum world, `CodeBot` and `Por` are fixed enum variants — can't be empty, can't be bot-only — so the checks are unreachable by construction.

**Why this is the right call:**
- icr's shape is the new world. Later commits in the chain (mvusyowm onward) all assume icr's 2-arg `plan_init` signature; reverting to main/ttzwvpoq's 1-arg form would cascade-break those.
- ttzwvpoq's *purpose* per its title is the Scope enum refactor; preserving that means preserving the `Scope(...)` → `Scope::Roles(...)` rename, which is mechanical at the call sites.
- The dropped validation isn't lost — it's obsoleted by the type system. `ScopeKind::CodeBot` and `ScopeKind::Por` are exhaustive variants; a malformed `--scope=bot` input is rejected at the parser level, not in plan_init.

If main's structure had been preferred, every downstream commit (mvusyowm, plyvnpxt, etc.) would have a much larger conflict surface — they all assume icr's shape. Going icr-structure-with-ttzwvpoq-rename is the path with the smallest cascade.

pub(crate) fn plan_init(
    args: &InitArgs,
    cfg: &UserConfig,
) -> Result<InitPlan, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
    debug!(
        "init args: target={:?}, name={:?}, account={:?}, repo={:?}, scope={:?}, private={}",
        args.target,
        args.name,
        args.account.account,
        args.repo.repo,
        args.scope.scope,
        args.provision.private.private
    );

    let scope = match args.scope.scope {
        ScopeKind::CodeBot => Scope(vec![Side::Code, Side::Bot]),
        ScopeKind::Por => Scope(vec![Side::Code]),
    };

    if scope.is_code_only()
        && let Some(t) = &args.use_template.use_template
        && t.contains(',')
    {
        return Err(format!(
            "--scope=por takes a single template path; got '{t}' (drop the `,BOT` half)"
        )
        .into());
    }

    if args.config.raw.is_some() && args.scope.scope == ScopeKind::CodeBot {
        return Err(
            "--config is only valid with --scope=por (dual-mode configs are per-side and unconditional)".into(),
        );
    }
    if let Some(ConfigKind::Path(p)) = args.config.resolve(ConfigKind::None)
        && !p.exists()
    {
        return Err(format!("--config: path does not exist: {}", p.display()).into());
    }

    let parsed = parse_target(&args.target)?;
    debug!("parse_target: {:?} → {:?}", args.target, parsed);
    let plan = match parsed {
        Target::Url(url) => plan_from_url(args, scope, url),

            plan_from_url(args, scope, format!("git@github.com:{o}/{n}.git"))

pub(crate) fn plan_init(args: &InitArgs) -> Result<InitPlan, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
    // --- Resolve scope first: default (None) → dual; empty Vec is
    // invalid; `bot` alone is meaningless at init time. ---
    let scope = match &args.scope {
        None => Scope::Roles(vec![Side::Code, Side::Bot]),
        Some(sides) => {
            let s = Scope::Roles(sides.clone());
            if s.is_empty() {
                return Err("--scope: value is empty".into());
            }
            if s.is_bot_only() {
                return Err(
                    "--scope=bot is meaningless at init time — use --scope=code for single-repo or --scope=code,bot (default) for dual-repo"
                        .into(),
                );
            }
            s
        }
    };

    // --- Mutual-exclusion gates (flag-pair ambiguity) ---
    if args.repo_local.is_some() && args.repo_remote.is_some() {
        return Err("--repo-local and --repo-remote are mutually exclusive".into());
    }
    if args.repo_local.is_some() && args.dir.is_some() {
        return Err(
            "--repo-local and --dir are mutually exclusive (--repo-local is the parent)".into(),
        );
    }
    if args.repo_local.is_some() && args.owner.is_some() {
        return Err(
            "--repo-local and --owner are mutually exclusive (fixture mode has no GitHub)".into(),
        );
    }
    if args.repo_local.is_some() && args.private {
        return Err(
            "--repo-local and --private are mutually exclusive (fixture mode has no GitHub)".into(),
        );
    }
    if args.repo_remote.is_some() && args.owner.is_some() {
        return Err(
            "--repo-remote and --owner are mutually exclusive (URL already encodes owner)".into(),
```

/// Build an `InitPlan` from CLI args + user config.
///
/// Dispatches on the parsed `<TARGET>` form:
///
/// - `Url(u)` / `OwnerName(o, n)` → URL is explicit; config not
///   consulted; `--account` and `--repo` are rejected as
///   meaningless.
/// - `Path(p)` → `p` is the destination; basename names the repo;
///   remote URL resolved from config via the `--repo` chain.
/// - `BareName(n)` → destination at `cwd/<n>`; remote resolved
///   from config.
///
/// The `cfg` parameter is the loaded user config; `init` loads it
/// once at entry and passes it through so tests can supply a
/// synthetic config without touching disk.
pub(crate) fn plan_init(
    args: &InitArgs,
    cfg: &UserConfig,
) -> Result<InitPlan, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
    debug!(
        "init args: target={:?}, name={:?}, account={:?}, repo={:?}, scope={:?}, private={}",
        args.target,
        args.name,
        args.account.account,
        args.repo.repo,
        args.scope.scope,
        args.provision.private.private
    );

    let scope = match args.scope.scope {
        ScopeKind::CodeBot => Scope::Roles(vec![Side::Code, Side::Bot]),
        ScopeKind::Por => Scope::Roles(vec![Side::Code]),
```

Proposed cargo cycle. Single chained command — stops at first failure, surfaces clean signal at success:

```
cargo fmt --all && cargo clippy --all-targets --all-features -- -D warnings && cargo test && cargo install --path . --locked
```

This runs:
1. `cargo fmt --all` (formats in place; should be no-op since resolutions copied known-formatted source)
2. `cargo clippy --all-targets --all-features -- -D warnings` (treats warnings as errors)
3. `cargo test` (full test suite)
4. `cargo install --path . --locked` (validates lockfile + produces vc-x1 binary at 0.42.0-1)

Approve? Will set a 10-minute timeout for install's release build.

Clippy flagged a real issue. `is_bot_only` and `is_empty` are defined on `Scope` but unused in ttzwvpoq's post-rebase state — because we dropped main's `Some(sides)` validation arm (which was the only non-test caller). icr's plan_init dispatches on `ScopeKind` enum and doesn't need them.

Need to verify these methods get used in downstream commits. If they do, the right fix is `#[allow(dead_code)]` mirroring the `Single` variant pattern in scope.rs (lines 39-42). If they don't, the methods should be removed entirely.

> DEVELOPER

Should we push this?

> AGENT

Strong recommendation: **no, don't push yet.** Three reasons:

1. **`main` is currently at a conflicted commit (`uxymmmyn`).** jj git push refuses to send commits with unresolved conflicts. There's nothing to push that wouldn't error or break the remote.

2. **Force-pushing a partially-resolved state cements incomplete work on origin.** Right now `main@origin` still has the canonical pre-rebase main — that's our cleanest recovery anchor. If something goes wrong with the local resolution, `jj git fetch && jj bookmark set main -r main@origin --allow-backwards` rewinds us to known-good. Once we force-push, that anchor is gone.

3. **The plan was always "single force-push at end of rebase."** From `~/rebase-status.md`: "After all 6 descendants are clean: cargo cycle (fmt, clippy, test, install --locked), then **force-push main** (the deferred hard-to-reverse step)." That structure is right — it makes the force-push a *deliberate* cycle-level event, not 5+ thrash-pushes through intermediate states.

The per-step push cadence in CLAUDE.md applies to forward-progress cycles (each step adds new work that should land on origin promptly). A rebase is the opposite shape: it rewrites already-pushed history, and intermediate commits are local mid-flight states that aren't meant to be visible on origin until the whole rewrite stabilizes.

**What is worth doing right now**: nothing more for safety. We already have:
- `~/rebase-status.md` (resume note outside repo).
- `main-2` bookmark (local pre-rebase snapshot at different chids — diff-comparison fallback).
- `main@origin` (canonical pre-rebase, untouched until force-push).
- `../vc-x1-20260505-1/` filesystem snapshot (heavy hammer, per the status file).
- `../vc-x1-main/` and `../vc-x1-icr/` reference clones.

Plenty of recovery surface area. Recommend continuing the resolution loop. Still want to advance to `mvusyowm`?

63	## Workflow pattern (per remaining conflicted commit)
64	
65	1. `jj new rslv-commit -R .` — fresh empty WC on top of the
66	   conflicted commit; working copy fills with conflict markers.
67	2. Read the conflicts, plan resolutions.
68	3. Edit conflict markers in working-copy files.
69	4. `jj resolve --list -R .` — verify no markers remain.
70	5. `jj squash -R .` — fold WC's resolution into the conflicted
71	   commit. Auto-rebases descendants. Some downstream conflicts
72	   may clear automatically.
73	6. `jj bookmark set rslv-commit -r <next-conflicted> -R .` —
74	   advance the cursor to the next still-conflicted commit.
75	7. Repeat from (1).
76	
77	After all 6 descendants are clean: cargo cycle (fmt, clippy, test,
78	install --locked), then **force-push main** (the deferred hard-to-
79	reverse step).

81	## Strategic context (why we did it this way)
82	
83	- **Direction: rebase main onto init-clone-refactor** (not the
84	  reverse). Reason: ochid trailers — main's commits each carry
85	  `ochid: /.claude/<chid>` pointing at .claude commits, and
86	  .claude carries reverse pointers at main's chids. Rebase
87	  preserves chids, so both directions stay valid. Promoting
88	  `main-2` instead would have given main-2 new chids and broken
89	  .claude's reverse pointers.
90	- **Duplicate-then-rebase pattern (main-2)** for safety.
91	  `main-2` is a local jj-level snapshot with different chids; it
92	  doesn't auto-follow when main is rewritten. Useful for `jj
93	  diff main..main-2` comparison if a resolution looks wrong.
94	- **Cycle structure (~30 commits) preserved**, no squash. We
95	  considered squashing main first to reduce conflict surfaces
96	  but decided commit-by-commit resolution preserves history
97	  granularity and the cascade often clears trivial conflicts
98	  automatically.
99	
100	## Recovery options (in escalating severity)
101	
102	1. `jj op restore <pre-rebase-op>` — local op-log undo. Rewinds
103	   to before the rebase started. First line of defense.
104	2. `jj git fetch` + `jj bookmark set main -r main@origin
105	   --allow-backwards` — origin still has canonical pre-rebase
106	   main until we force-push. Second line.
107	3. `cp -a ../vc-x1-20260505-1/. .` — filesystem copy from
108	   `~/data/prgs/rust/vc-x1-20260505-1/`. Heavy hammer; restores
109	   everything including .claude state.
110	4. Re-clone from origin. Nuclear option.
111	
112	`main-2` is a fourth fallback (local jj snapshot with different
113	chids), but only useful as a comparison point — promoting it
114	would break .claude's reverse ochids as discussed above.
115	
116	## Notes for resuming
117	
118	- Conflicts visible in the WC are jj's 3-way format. `<<<<<<<`
119	  and `>>>>>>>` delimit conflict regions. `%%%%%%%` introduces a
120	  diff section (BASE → SIDE differential); `+++++++` introduces
121	  a snapshot (literal content of one side). One side typically
122	  shown as diff (compact for small changes), the other as
123	  snapshot.
124	- Per-commit conflicts are typically smaller than the cumulative
125	  conflict at main's tip — descendants inherit prior cascades.
126	  `jj resolve --list -r main` is the cumulative view; `-r
127	  rslv-commit` is the current commit's specific surface.
128	- Cargo.toml / Cargo.lock cleared automatically after ozvrpkrx
129	  was resolved — auto-rebase applied per-commit version-bump
130	  diffs cleanly onto the resolved 0.42.0-0 base.
